Clockwise literal יטאאה; full glyph reversal האאטי. Counterclockwise from the SAME starting cell gives יט | ה | א | א -> יטהאא. These are different transformations, retained distinctly.
Returning to the starting cell would emit another יט if every visit emits text. That repeated visit is not counted in the four-distinct-cell sum.

All possible 81x49 native 2x2 placements scanned; 88 have four nonzero source cells. Exactly one such block sums to26: top-left r62c02.

Entity A: single observed cell כו at r60c02. State: additive numeral26.

Previously traced route אגאאדביד also totals26, but this equality depends on its chosen start/end. Removing its initial א gives25. The lower loop comparison is explicitly a bounded four-cell sum; neither equality establishes semantic equivalence.

The loop cells, values and coordinates are recoverable from the original grids. The value equality survives orientation reversal because addition is commutative; therefore that invariance alone does not establish direction.
Full reversal does change the letter strings, so word meaning cannot be inferred from preserved totals. This limits the value relation without discarding the paths.

Numerology trial001 — interpretation notes after inspecting the complete ledger
Atbash expression candidate: ביטא -> שמנת. שמנת is an attested noun cream/sour cream: https://www.pealim.com/dict/3301-shamenet/ . This is a new generated lexical match, not evidence selecting the cipher or a coherent message.
Milui example under default He=הא, Vav=ואו: כו -> כף | ואו -> 113. Expansion is spelling out names, not recovering source glyphs.
Comparison across three recorded candidates:
{"id": "well", "input": "היטב", "standard": 26, "ordinal": 26, "katan_sum": 17, "default_milui_parts": ["הא", "יוד", "טית", "בית"], "default_milui_value": 857, "all_milui_totals": [857, 861, 866]}
{"id": "single_26_cell", "input": "כו", "standard": 26, "ordinal": 17, "katan_sum": 8, "default_milui_parts": ["כף", "ואו"], "default_milui_value": 113, "all_milui_totals": [112, 113, 122]}
{"id": "lower_26_loop", "input": "יטאאה", "standard": 26, "ordinal": 26, "katan_sum": 17, "default_milui_parts": ["יוד", "טית", "אלף", "אלף", "הא"], "default_milui_value": 667, "all_milui_totals": [667, 671, 676]}
היטב and lower loop יטאאה both total26 standard,26 ordinal,17 per-letter reduced. These are overlapping low-valued source glyphs; the arithmetic systems are not independent confirmations. Their default milui totals857 and667 differ.
The single כו and loop have equal standard26 and equal total digital-root8, but unequal ordinal, per-letter reduced, Atbash-valued, Albam-valued, and default milui results. Equal digital roots follow automatically from equal standard totals.
All initials/endings outputs are retained in the ledger, with no invented acronym phrases. None currently supplies a demonstrated continuation.
No source glyphs or zeros replaced. All candidate transformations remain additional text below the original full streams.

Greek trial001 — Hebrew-root comparison and orthographic-role audit
Root extraction is a separate dictionary-supported morphological layer, not deletion of source letters. Original full forms and all occurrence traces remain above.
{"hebrew_candidate": "ביטא", "hebrew_root": "בטא", "mapped_root": "ΒΘΑ", "root_role": "expression / pronunciation", "greek_translation_lemma": "εκφράζω", "greek_word_family": "ἐκ- + φράζω; φραζ-/φρασ-", "source": "https://www.pealim.com/dict/209-levate/"}
{"hebrew_candidate": "היטב", "hebrew_root": "יטב", "mapped_root": "ΙΘΒ", "root_role": "goodness / doing well", "greek_translation_lemma": "καλῶς; καλός family", "greek_word_family": "καλ-", "source": "https://www.pealim.com/dict/737-lehetiv/"}
{"hebrew_candidate": "וידא", "hebrew_root": "ודא", "mapped_root": "ϜΔΑ", "root_role": "making sure / verification", "greek_translation_lemma": "ελέγχω (selected checking equivalent)", "greek_word_family": "ἐλεγχ-", "source": "https://www.pealim.com/he/dict/479-levade/"}
The shared source יט is orthographically identical across the candidate word paths, but its yod has different morphological roles: in ביטא the lexical root is בטא and yod belongs to the unpointed spelling; in היטב the root is יטב, so yod is a root radical. In וידא, yod is likewise absent from the root ודא.
Thus the candidate forms share letters without being the same Hebrew root. Do not treat their Greek carrier overlap ΙΘ as proof of an etymological relationship.
Root-level Book II interpretation: ΒΘΑ = branch / constraint / origin; ΙΘΒ = instance / constraint / branch; ϜΔΑ = retention / change / origin. These are selected Greek operator projections of Hebrew radicals, not Greek lexical roots.
Possible connection to pursue: preserve the identity of a source constituent while its grammatical or relational role changes across paths. This fits the recorded shared occurrence and the Book II retention/change distinction; it does not by itself select a message.
No original source glyph removed, added or altered. Root morphology, semantic translation and Book II operator projection remain explicitly separate.

============================================================
Corrected three-way comparison — one table per word with complete calculable totals

User format: all methods together within a separate table per Hebrew word. No Greek lexical match by whole-word numerical total is substituted for meaning translation.
Numerical rules: use ordinary Hebrew values and Greek alphabetic numeral values; replace EACH Hebrew letter at the same value. Whole-word and whole-section totals are rendered separately in descending additive Greek numerals. DigammaϜ/StigmaϚ are displayed alternatives for6. No Book II operator readings applied.
Original encoder audit: WOWSignal/scripts/step03_hebrew_gematria.py maps each source intensity cell independently; it does not store five-byte grouping boundaries. To perform a bounded full-section calculation without inventing that history, enumerate EVERY linear five-reading window containing the exact source cells participating in each candidate word, including observed zeros.
Word paths that turn across axes cannot be enclosed by a single linear window. Their actual participating-cell sums are calculated explicitly, not left blank. Choosing a new five-cell turning walk would be a further hypothesis, not an original source section.
Five source characters are ASCII bytes in the printout transcription. Their intensity values and raw ASCII code values differ; both interpretations of the two core five-character sections are explicitly retained below.
All listed Greek translation options are semantic candidates from the previously identified senses, not an exhaustive claim over every contextual paraphrase.
Whole-word totals are shown in addition to, and are never passed off as, original five-reading totals.

============================================================
Coherent-reading comparison — source continuity, translation, and new tilt partition

Comparison axes: linguistic fluency, preservation of observed glyph order, number of editorial changes, grammatical ambiguity. This is qualitative comparison, not numerical likelihood or evidence of intentional encoding.

New source-preserving partition:
הטילוכדיו = הטי | לו | כדיו
Tilt his/its jugs for him/it (feminine singular addressee). The Greek translation is a meaning rendering, not a numeric-symbol replacement.
Every one of the9 reversed glyph occurrences is retained once. Source span: channel02, rows63 down to58, reversing letters inside multi-letter tokens as previously defined.
Forward tail יטה = he/it will incline/tilt (Hifil candidate); reverse head הטי = incline/tilt! to a woman. Both belong to נטה under the selected lexical reading. This is a new root-level connection, not an insertion of an otherwise absent word.
Both vessel interpretations remain grammatically elliptical: modern Hebrew would commonly insert את before the definite object כדיו. No את has been added to the source or claimed as recovered.
Lexical sources: https://www.pealim.com/dict/1193-lehatot/ ; https://www.pealim.com/dict/739-lehatil/ ; https://www.pealim.com/he/dict/7880-kad/

Most source-faithful multiword alternatives: T1-T3, all derived by partitioning the same continuous reverse run. T1 additionally preserves the forward/reverse tilt root relationship. This makes it worth pursuing, not uniquely selected or proven.

============================================================
Vessel vocabulary expansion — כדיו beyond the narrow English word jugs

User requests broader alternatives to jugs, focusing on container.
Preserve original Hebrew כדיו and existing readings; do not silently replace every previous gloss.
Direct lexical range of כד: jar, pitcher, jug, vessel. Jastrow describes a rounded/pouched vessel and gives uses for carrying/holding liquids and contents. כדיו retains plural and third-masculine-singular possession: his/its vessels.
Broad English translation candidates: his/its vessels; his/its jars; his/its storage vessels (storage is a functional qualification); his/its containers (broader abstraction). Receptacles is another functional paraphrase.
Carrier or holder describes a container function, not a separately attested lexical meaning of כד in this context.
Data container, buffer, memory, body, or receiver would be contextual/metaphorical hypotheses, not direct translations recovered from the letters. No one of those is selected in this pass.
Revised working candidate, retaining original uncertainty: הטי | לו | כדיו -> Tilt his/its vessels for him/it. Broader process paraphrase: orient the containers for him/it. Orient is an interpretive broadening of tilt, not a new recovered word.
Greek semantic options: δοχεία for containers/receptacles; αγγεία for vessels (context-dependent); κάδοι for bins/buckets/vessels. Greek κάδος is an attested vessel term and a useful lexical comparison, not the equal-value Greek rendering ΚΔ of Hebrew כד.
Sources: https://daf-yomi.com/Dictionary.aspx?id=8&wordId=62953 ; https://www.pealim.com/dict/7880-kad/ ; https://www.greek-language.gr/greekLang/modern_greek/tools/lexica/search.html?lq=κάδος
No source characters changed. This pass broadens recorded translation alternatives; it does not establish an intended transmitted referent.

3. Execute textual return precisely:
Chosen reverse reading: הטי | לו | כדיו.
Reverse EACH word and reverse word order: וידכ | ול | יטה.

For competing הטילו | כדיו, the exact return grouping is וידכ | וליטה. It also returns to the same9 source glyphs. Round-trip identity therefore does not select one word partition.

4. Follow the root relationship:
Forward final יטה = he/it will incline/tilt (candidate Hifil); reverse initial הטי = incline/tilt! to a woman. Both are dictionary-supported forms of root נטה.
Lexical link: orientation/inclination. What changes on reversal is grammatical form and addressed actor; the same source occurrences are reused.
Remaining forward grouping וידכ | ול is not a translated sentence. Alternative complete partition ו | יד | כול | יטה supplies and | hand | all | will tilt, but its syntax and agreement remain unresolved. No deleted letters or inserted subject/object are used to claim resolution.

5. Follow the vessel/container relationship:
Container role: distinguish the vessel from its contents, retain identity and possession, permit changed orientation. Actual contents, opening and physical transfer are not observed in these source readings.
Conditional physical hypothesis: tilting an open vessel containing fluid can change where contents go. Pour/empty/transfer are consequences to test if the needed context appears; none is a word recovered in this pass.
Process hypothesis: changing a container orientation while preserving what is tracked. A computational container, buffer, packet, receiver, body or spacecraft remains a context choice, not a direct translation of כדיו.
Greek semantic comparison: δοχείο/δοχή belongs to the receiving/holding family associated with δέχομαι. This supports receptacle as a functional description, but is the etymology of the chosen Greek translation, not a newly discovered Hebrew root or numeric equivalence.

6. Coherence outcome:
Leading physically coherent candidate for this continuous run: Tilt his/its vessels for him/it.
Leading broader process paraphrase: Orient his/its containers for him/it.
Tilt is supported by the exact reverse-root connection. Orient is an explicit broader interpretation. Vessel/containers avoids the overly specific English image of a jug, while leaving the intended referent open.
The expression/verification candidates ביטא / היטב / וידא can be compared thematically (action, quality, checking) but are not appended as if they follow this continuous run. Doing so would change paths and reuse letters.
No complete new forward sentence or intended message established. Textual reversal is not evidence that a physical operation has been undone.

============================================================
Encoded-container hypothesis — source-token boundaries and reversible five-bit trials

User hypothesis: unfamiliar words might contain compressed or encoded payload, rather than ordinary vocabulary. Retain this model and test the underlying unit before assigning more word glosses.

1. Known encoding layer (our representation, not proof of an encoded transmission):
{"source_values": [6, 14, 26, 30, 19, 5], "hebrew_tokens": ["ו", "יד", "כו", "ל", "יט", "ה"], "source_rows": [58, 63], "channel": 2}
Each source reading has a token boundary. 14 -> יד;26 -> כו;19 -> יט. Six source readings become nine Hebrew glyph occurrences. This is additive numeral expansion, not compression at this layer.
Token-level 14 -> יד ->10+4 ->14 is reversible with the original boundaries. An unsegmented string alone does not retain those boundaries.

2. Test the container hypothesis against word partitions:
Full glyph reverse: ה | טי | ל | וכ | די | ו. Candidate words: הטי | לו | כדיו.
לו uses ל from bin30 and ו from inside bin26; כדיו starts with כ from that SAME bin26. The word boundary therefore cuts an original source token into two.
If source readings are payload units, cross-token Hebrew word partitions might be accidental. The container model gives a reason to test original unit boundaries instead of assuming unknown strings must be words.
Reverse source containers only: ה | יט | ל | כו | יד | ו -> היטלכוידו.
Reverse containers AND internal glyph order: ה | טי | ל | וכ | די | ו -> הטילוכדיו. These are different operations, not interchangeable readings.

3. Bounded binary test:
B5 hypothesis: each observed bin carries a five-bit payload symbol. Each of the six values fits0..31, giving30 bits total. This is a chosen test width, not recorded instrument bit depth; it is not a five-byte block.
Tried two reading directions, two within-reading bit orders, and eight byte offsets =32 layouts. Every layout preserves all leading/trailing bits and round-trips to its full bit string and six original bins.
No all-printable three-byte output occurred. One two-byte slice reads /[ under reverse reading order, LSB-first per reading, offset7; fourteen other bits remain explicitly retained. It is not treated as a decoded word.
RFC4648 five-bit alphabet display: GO26TF forward; FT62OG reverse. This is a symbol-index display, not a demonstrated canonical byte encoding; padding was not invented.
No compression algorithm identified or successfully applied. Encoding and compression remain distinct hypotheses.
Encoding reference: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4648.html

4. Connection to orientation:
Operational hypothesis: preserve each source unit as a container while changing orientation. Test unit order separately from internal glyph/bit order, with exact inverses. This is a proposed method, not an instruction established by the source.
Forward unresolved וידכ | ול | יטה remains intact. No extra Hebrew letter, missing key, algorithm name, or complete payload is claimed.
Execution note: an initial pre-write assertion incorrectly assumed no fully printable slice. It failed on /[ before any file write. The assertion and summary were corrected; the result is retained, not discarded.
